As Bayan’s ethical explanation, captivity and another person’s control over your freedom make an appeal to free choice deeply problematic. Describing the legal category does not independently demonstrate morally adequate consent. This draft does not invent a universal consent requirement that these particular texts do not state. [Ibn Kathir][ik236]

**Historical experience needs historical evidence.** A 1938–1939 Gulf file preserves a translated petition from an enslaved woman who fled with her daughters because she feared their sale. It documents her reported predicament in that setting, not every Muslim society. The account reaches us through British officials and an archival transcription with possible errors; it is not an unmediated recording or a religious ruling. [British Library record, folio 191r][qdl191]

**Today’s human-rights standard is explicit.** Article 4 of the Universal Declaration prohibits slavery and the slave trade. This is a modern rights document, not a Quran commentary. Studying older texts confers no ownership over a worker, captive or anyone else. A fuller account of abolition in particular countries requires their own records; this article does not invent one date for the whole Muslim world. [Universal Declaration, Article 4][udhr4]

**Bayan’s guide:** Keep four questions separate: what the texts permit, what they restrict, what people actually experienced, and what ethical defence is being offered. If your objection is that a human being should not be owned at all, say that directly. Emancipation provisions matter, but listing them alone does not answer that objection. This draft provides an honest starting explanation, not an exhaustive moral justification or a contemporary permission to enslave.

**Follow-up answers**

- **Was an enslaved person simply an employee?** No. The distinction between a free person and someone sold, and the provisions for emancipation, describe a different status. [Bukhari 2227][b2227], [Quran 24:33][q2433]
- **Does encouragement to free slaves prove immediate abolition?** No. Encouraging release and recognising continuing ownership are different claims. Both appear in the cited material. [Quran 90:13][q9013], [23:5–6][q235]
- **Can we say every captive freely consented?** These sources do not establish that. The Awtaas report addresses legal permissibility, not a documented account of each woman’s agreement. It cannot carry a claim it does not demonstrate. [Muslim 1456a][m1456a]

**Continue:** [Religious freedom](#religious-freedom) · [Harm and abuse](#quran-4-34) · [Spread by the sword?](#sword)

### العربية: كيف نفهم الرق في النصوص والتاريخ الإسلامي؟

**الجواب الواضح**

تقر النصوص الإسلامية الرق بوصفه ملكية فعلية للبشر، مع الحث على العتق وفرض قيود. ويضلل وصف هذه الحال بأنها عمل مأجور عادي أو القول إن الحث على التحرير ألغى الملك فوراً. تميز الآيتان 23:5–6 الأزواج وملك اليمين، ويفسر ابن كثير الأخير بالإماء من السبي في كلامه عن العلاقات الجنسية المباحة. [القرآن 23:5–6][q235]، [ابن كثير][ik236]

يثير ذلك سؤالاً أخلاقياً جاداً عن الحرية والرضا، خاصة حيث شملت الملكية إباحة العلاقة الجنسية. لا يجيب الاقتصار على حسن المعاملة عن هذا السؤال. على بيان عرض المؤسسة والقيود والاعتراضات معاً، دون تصور أن كل ممارسة تاريخية وافقت الأحكام الدينية أو أن التنظيم هو الإلغاء.

**لنفهم المسألة**

**التحرير تعليم إيجابي مع بقاء الملك ظاهراً.** تمدح الآية 90:13 فك الرقبة. وتأمر الآية 24:33 بالمكاتبة عند تحقق شرط الخير المذكور، وتنهى عن إكراه الإماء على البغاء، مع عبارة «إن أردن تحصناً» المذكورة. ويروي مسلم العتق كفارة للطْم المملوك أو ضربه، ويدين البخاري بيع الحر. هذه قيود وطرق للتحرير ذات معنى، لا دليل على زوال صنف الرقيق حينئذ. [القرآن 90:13][q9013]، [24:33][q2433]، [مسلم 1657a][m1657a]، [البخاري 2227][b2227]

**يجب تناول الإباحة الجنسية مباشرة.** يصف خبر مسلم في أوطاس سبايا لهن أزواج مشركون وتطبيق الآية 4:24 على العلاقة بعد مدة الانتظار المذكورة. لا يصف الخبر عقد زواج جديداً مع كل أسيرة ولا يثبت موافقة كل امرأة الحرة. لا يصح جعل النهي عن البغاء دليلاً على طوعية جميع العلاقات في الملك. ولا يثبت غياب وصف الرضا تطابق تجربة كل فرد. [مسلم 1456a][m1456a]، [القرآن 24:33][q2433]

**الإباحة وظروف الإكراه مسألتان مختلفتان.** يجعل التفسير المذكور الملك سبباً مشروعاً للعلاقة متميزاً عن النكاح. ومن شرح بيان الأخلاقي أن الأسر وسيطرة غيرك على حريتك يجعلان الاحتجاج بالاختيار الحر شديد الإشكال. لا يثبت وصف الباب الفقهي مستقلاً رضا كافياً أخلاقياً. لا تخترع المسودة شرط رضا عاماً لا تنص عليه هذه النصوص بعينها. [ابن كثير][ik236]

**تحتاج التجربة التاريخية إلى دليل تاريخي.** يحفظ ملف خليجي من 1938–1939 عريضة مترجمة لامرأة مسترقة هربت مع ابنتيها خوفاً من بيعهن. يوثق ما أبلغت عنه في تلك البيئة لا كل مجتمع مسلم. وصل الحساب عبر موظفين بريطانيين ونقل أرشيفي يحتمل أخطاء، وليس تسجيلاً مباشراً بلا وسيط ولا حكماً دينياً. [سجل المكتبة البريطانية، الورقة 191و][qdl191]

**المعيار الحقوقي الحديث صريح.** تحظر المادة الرابعة من الإعلان العالمي لحقوق الإنسان الرق وتجارته. هذه وثيقة حقوقية حديثة لا تفسير للقرآن. ولا تمنح دراسة النصوص القديمة ملكاً على عامل أو أسير أو غيره. يحتاج بحث إلغاء الرق في البلدان إلى سجلاتها الخاصة؛ فلا يخترع المقال تاريخاً واحداً للعالم الإسلامي كله. [الإعلان العالمي، المادة 4][udhr4]

**دليل بيان:** افصل أربعة أسئلة: ماذا تبيح النصوص، وماذا تقيد، وماذا عاش الناس فعلاً، وأي دفاع أخلاقي يقدم؟ إن كان اعتراضك هو ألا يملك إنسان أصلاً فاذكره مباشرة. تهم أحكام العتق، لكن تعدادها وحده لا يجيب عن الاعتراض. تقدم المسودة مدخلاً صادقاً، لا تبريراً أخلاقياً شاملاً ولا ترخيصاً حاضراً بالاسترقاق.

**أسئلة متابعة**

- **هل كان المسترق موظفاً فحسب؟** لا. يصف الفرق بين الحر والمبيع وأحكام العتق وضعاً مختلفاً. [البخاري 2227][b2227]، [القرآن 24:33][q2433]
- **هل يثبت الحث على العتق إلغاء فورياً؟** لا. التشجيع على التحرير والإقرار ببقاء الملك دعويان مختلفتان، وتظهران معاً في المادة المذكورة. [القرآن 90:13][q9013]، [23:5–6][q235]
- **هل نستطيع القول إن كل أسيرة رضيت بحرية؟** لا تثبت المصادر ذلك. يبحث خبر أوطاس الإباحة الفقهية لا وصفاً موثقاً لموافقة كل امرأة. فلا يحمل دعوى لم يثبتها. [مسلم 1456a][m1456a]

**للمتابعة:** [حرية الدين](#religious-freedom) · [الأذى والإساءة](#quran-4-34) · [هل انتشر بالسيف؟](#sword)

### Deutsch: Wie ist Sklaverei in islamischen Texten und Geschichte zu verstehen?

**Die klare Antwort**

Islamische Texte erkennen Sklaverei als tatsächliches Eigentum an Menschen an und empfehlen zugleich Freilassung und setzen Grenzen. Dies als gewöhnliche bezahlte Beschäftigung zu beschreiben oder aus der Empfehlung sofortige Abschaffung abzuleiten wäre irreführend. Quran 23:5–6 unterscheidet Ehefrauen von Menschen im Besitz; Ibn Kathir erklärt Letztere im Zusammenhang erlaubter sexueller Beziehungen als versklavte gefangene Frauen. [Quran 23:5–6][q235], [Ibn Kathir][ik236]

Das wirft eine ernsthafte moralische Frage nach Freiheit und Zustimmung auf, besonders wenn Eigentum sexuellen Zugang umfasste. Eine Antwort nur über gute Behandlung ließe diese Frage offen. Bayan sollte Institution, Grenzen und Einwände gemeinsam darstellen, ohne jede historische Praxis als regelkonform oder Regulierung als Abschaffung auszugeben.

**Die Frage genauer verstehen**

**Befreiung ist positiv, Eigentum bleibt aber sichtbar.** Quran 90:13 lobt Freilassung. Quran 24:33 verlangt Freilassungsverträge bei der dort genannten Voraussetzung des erkannten Guten und verbietet das Zwingen versklavter Frauen zur Prostitution, einschließlich der genannten Wendung „wenn sie keusch bleiben wollen“. Muslim nennt Freilassung als Sühne für Ohrfeigen oder Schlagen eines versklavten Menschen; Bukhari verurteilt den Verkauf eines freien Menschen. Das sind bedeutsame Grenzen und Befreiungswege, kein Beleg dafür, dass der Sklavenstatus bereits aufgehört hätte. [Quran 90:13][q9013], [24:33][q2433], [Muslim 1657a][m1657a], [Bukhari 2227][b2227]

**Die sexuelle Bestimmung braucht eine direkte Antwort.** Muslims Bericht über Awtaas beschreibt Gefangene mit bereits bestehenden polytheistischen Ehemännern und die Anwendung von Quran 4:24 auf Beziehungen nach der genannten Wartezeit. Er beschreibt weder mit jeder Gefangenen einen neuen Ehevertrag noch belegt er die freie Zustimmung jeder Frau. Das Prostitutionsverbot beweist nicht, dass alle Beziehungen unter Eigentum freiwillig waren. Fehlende Zustimmungsberichte beweisen umgekehrt nicht, dass jede individuelle Erfahrung identisch war. [Muslim 1456a][m1456a], [Quran 24:33][q2433]

**Erlaubnis und Zwangslage sind unterschiedliche Fragen.** Die zitierte Auslegung behandelt Eigentum als erlaubte, von Ehe verschiedene Grundlage für Beziehungen. Nach Bayans ethischer Erklärung machen Gefangenschaft und fremde Kontrolle über die eigene Freiheit die Berufung auf freie Wahl äußerst problematisch. Die Rechtskategorie zu beschreiben belegt nicht unabhängig eine moralisch ausreichende Zustimmung. Dieser Entwurf erfindet keine allgemeine Zustimmungsvoraussetzung, die diese besonderen Texte nicht nennen. [Ibn Kathir][ik236]

**Historische Erfahrungen brauchen historische Belege.** Eine Golfakte von 1938–1939 bewahrt eine übersetzte Petition einer versklavten Frau, die mit ihren Töchtern aus Angst vor ihrem Verkauf floh. Sie dokumentiert ihre geschilderte Lage in diesem Umfeld, nicht jede muslimische Gesellschaft. Der Bericht wurde durch britische Beamte vermittelt und liegt als möglicherweise fehlerhafte Archivtranskription vor; er ist weder eine unvermittelte Aufnahme noch ein religiöses Urteil. [British Library, Blatt 191r][qdl191]

**Der moderne Menschenrechtsstandard ist ausdrücklich.** Artikel 4 der Allgemeinen Erklärung der Menschenrechte verbietet Sklaverei und Sklavenhandel. Das ist ein modernes Rechtsdokument, kein Qurankommentar. Alte Texte zu studieren verleiht kein Eigentum an Arbeitskräften, Gefangenen oder anderen Menschen. Die Abschaffung in einzelnen Ländern braucht deren eigene Akten; dieser Artikel erfindet kein gemeinsames Datum für die gesamte muslimische Welt. [Allgemeine Erklärung, Artikel 4][udhr4]

**Bayans Orientierung:** Trenne vier Fragen: Was erlauben die Texte, was begrenzen sie, was erlebten Menschen tatsächlich, und welche ethische Verteidigung wird angeboten? Lautet dein Einwand, dass kein Mensch überhaupt besessen werden sollte, benenne ihn direkt. Freilassungsregeln sind bedeutsam; sie nur aufzulisten beantwortet den Einwand nicht. Dieser Entwurf bietet einen ehrlichen Einstieg, keine umfassende moralische Rechtfertigung oder heutige Erlaubnis zur Versklavung.

**Weitere Fragen**

- **War ein versklavter Mensch einfach ein Angestellter?** Nein. Die Unterscheidung von freien und verkauften Personen und die Freilassungsbestimmungen beschreiben einen anderen Status. [Bukhari 2227][b2227], [Quran 24:33][q2433]
- **Beweist die Empfehlung zur Freilassung sofortige Abschaffung?** Nein. Befreiung zu fördern und fortbestehendes Eigentum anzuerkennen sind verschiedene Aussagen. Beide erscheinen im zitierten Material. [Quran 90:13][q9013], [23:5–6][q235]
- **Können wir freie Zustimmung jeder Gefangenen behaupten?** Diese Quellen belegen das nicht. Der Awtaas-Bericht behandelt rechtliche Erlaubnis, keine dokumentierte Zustimmung jeder Frau. Er kann keine Behauptung tragen, die er nicht nachweist. [Muslim 1456a][m1456a]

**Weiterlesen:** [Religionsfreiheit](#religious-freedom) · [Schaden und Misshandlung](#quran-4-34) · [Verbreitung durch das Schwert?](#sword)

**Claim-to-source map:** [q235](https://quran.com/23/5-6) and [ik236](https://quran.com/23:6/tafsirs/en-tafisr-ibn-kathir) establish wives versus ownership and the sexual interpretation. [q9013](https://quran.com/90/13) commends release; [q2433](https://quran.com/24/33) includes conditional emancipation contracts and prostitution prohibition, not a universal proof of voluntary sex. [m1657a](https://sunnah.com/muslim:1657a) supplies expiation; [b2227](https://sunnah.com/bukhari:2227) condemns selling a free person. [m1456a](https://sunnah.com/muslim:1456a) explicitly addresses married captives after waiting, not each woman’s consent. [qdl191](https://www.qdl.qa/en/archive/81055/vdc_100106170204.0x0000c1) supplies the translated flight/sale-fear petition, with mediation/transcription limits. [udhr4](https://www.un.org/en/node/124305) supplies modern rights prohibition only, not a religious interpretation or full national-law survey.

**Editorial status:** Drafted in EN/AR/DE; source relevance and meaning compared editorially. Independent acceptance remains open.

**Unresolved points and review limits:** No exhaustive legal-school consent analysis, full moral justification of ownership, or country-by-country abolition history. The archival statement is mediated and transcription may err. Independent scholarly and native-language acceptance remain open.

## Expansion briefs and current status

The original nine briefs are retained as the research record, not evidence of measured demand. Status distinguishes completed local drafts from unfinished candidates. Full answers and current unresolved points appear in the articles above.

### 1. Why Islam rather than another religion?

**Reader concern:** Why should a thoughtful person accept Islam’s particular truth claims when other religions also offer purpose, morality and spiritual experience?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Articles 2 and 5 clarify Creator reasoning and evidence. They do not establish Islam rather than competing revelations. New to Islam introduces beliefs but does not substitute for this comparative question.

**Original research brief:** Use the actual foundational texts of each tradition being compared, with named translations and context; specify which claim is being tested. Research the Quran’s claims and arguments, including [4:82](https://quran.com/4/82), their approved tafsir and objections. Separate the existence of a Creator, prophethood and textual transmission. Prepare a fair comparison rubric before selecting examples.

**Research difficulties:** A religious text’s own assertion is not independent authentication for a reader who has not accepted it. Avoid caricatures, claims about all believers, selective comparisons and unsupported historical claims. A full survey of every religion would exceed one article; define the comparisons openly.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 2. What evidence supports Muhammad’s prophethood?

**Reader concern:** What makes belief in Muhammad as a messenger reasonable, beyond admiration or growing up Muslim?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** New to Islam lesson 4 explains his role; articles 5, 7 and 8 explain evidence, Quran transmission and hadith. None is a full case for prophethood.

**Original research brief:** Build a claim table distinguishing Quranic arguments, authenticated reports, contemporary/early historical evidence and later biography. Begin with [4:82](https://quran.com/4/82) as an argument requiring explanation and [18:110](https://quran.com/18/110) as the stated role, not independent proof. Research specific miracle and character reports with grades, exact wording and named scholarly explanations; evaluate each proposed inference.

**Research difficulties:** Avoid circular proof, ungraded popular stories and invented predictions. Historical reports have different evidential strengths. Distinguish the case for sincerity from the case for revelation; address alternative explanations fairly without pretending one anecdote settles everything.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 3. If Allah knows and decrees everything, do our choices matter?

**Reader concern:** Am I really responsible if Allah already knows what I will do and has decreed events? Why try?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 1 addresses responsibility and purpose, article 3 suffering. New to Islam lesson 7 names decree without settling freedom and accountability.

**Original research brief:** Read [81:28–29](https://quran.com/81/28-29) together, preserving both human willing and dependence on Allah. Research the full decree reports in Muslim 8a and the reports about acting rather than relying on destiny; verify exact passages and approved explanations from Ibn Baz or Ibn Uthaymeen. Separate knowledge, willing, creation and coercion before explaining their relationships.

**Research difficulties:** An analogy about foreknowledge alone does not address the whole doctrine of decree. Avoid both denying human responsibility and describing human choice as independent of Allah. Preserve the named explanation instead of inventing a philosophical reconciliation.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 4. Why does dua sometimes seem unanswered?

**Reader concern:** I asked sincerely and nothing changed. Was I ignored, did I do something wrong, or was the promise untrue?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 3 treats suffering; New to Islam lesson 18 teaches dua. This article should examine expectation, apparent nonresponse and evidence rather than repeat how to make dua.

**Original research brief:** Read [2:186](https://quran.com/2/186) in context and [Muslim 2735c](https://sunnah.com/muslim:2735c), including prohibited requests and impatience. Research the exact grading and wording of reports about different forms of response, rather than quoting an unchecked popular summary. Obtain approved commentary explaining the promise and its conditions.

**Research difficulties:** Do not blame a particular sufferer or claim to know why a request was not visibly granted. Do not promise a timetable, guaranteed material result or psychological cure. Distinguish authenticated teaching from practical emotional support.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 5. How should I understand eternal punishment and justice?

**Reader concern:** How can unending punishment be just for choices made in a finite life, especially with unequal access to understanding?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Articles 3 and 11 address justice and reaching the message. This question concerns duration, proportionality and the actual scope of punishment texts.

**Original research brief:** Map audiences and conditions in [4:168–169](https://quran.com/4/168-169), the justice principle in [4:40](https://quran.com/4/40), and other relevant Quran passages. Research authentic intercession reports and approved tafsir before distinguishing different categories of people and outcomes. Any attributed disagreement about duration needs its own exact primary scholarly passage.

**Research difficulties:** Avoid applying one punishment passage to every sinner, erasing explicit duration, or converting an attributed rationale into an exhaustive divine explanation. Do not pronounce individual relatives’ destinations. Philosophical proportionality objections need an actual response, not only a repeated assertion of justice.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 6. What does Quran 4:34 mean, particularly concerning harm and abuse?

**Reader concern:** Does the verse permit a husband to harm or control his wife, and how do its words and explanations relate to her safety and dignity?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 9 addresses spiritual standing and explicitly does not settle this passage. New to Islam modesty and family guidance do not cover marital harm.

**Original research brief:** Read the Arabic and named translations of [4:34–35](https://quran.com/an-nisa/34-35), not just one English rendering. Compare recognised tafsir and approved scholars on qiwamah, nushuz, the disputed phrase, restrictions and arbitration. Include [Muslim 2328a](https://sunnah.com/muslim:2328a) in its exact scope. Research reliable harm-related reports, named scholarly answers about injury and remedies, and suitable present-day support information if the article offers it.

**Research difficulties:** Do not silently replace a classical interpretation with a preferred translation, or treat a restriction as permission for abuse. Avoid turning an educational explanation into a personal instruction to remain in danger. Distinguish historical law, attributed interpretation and any jurisdiction-specific legal guidance, which needs current official evidence.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 7. How should questions about Aisha’s age and marriage be examined?

**Reader concern:** What do the reports actually say, how reliable are alternative chronologies, and how should the moral concern be addressed honestly?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 8 explains transmission methods, while New to Islam lesson 4 introduces the Prophet. Neither supplies this historical and ethical examination.

**Original research brief:** Begin with [Bukhari 5134](https://sunnah.com/bukhari:5134) and [Muslim 1422a](https://sunnah.com/muslim:1422a), distinguishing marriage contract and consummation. Trace chains, wording and named scholarly assessments. Test alternative age calculations against their own source dates and reliability. Historical customs or medical-development claims require suitable independent evidence.

**Research difficulties:** Do not conceal the reported ages, assume a speculative calculation disproves an authentic report, or invent statements about Aisha’s physical or psychological development. “It was normal then” requires evidence and does not alone answer the moral objection. Keep historical explanation distinct from present-day marriage guidance.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 8. How do religious freedom and classical apostasy rulings relate?

**Reader concern:** Can a person honestly examine belief if leaving carries a penalty in classical law? How does that relate to no compulsion?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 12 presents difficult material about conquest and coercion without settling apostasy. Article 4 distinguishes questions and unwanted thoughts from a religious-status judgment.

**Original research brief:** Read [2:256](https://quran.com/2/256), [Bukhari 6922](https://sunnah.com/bukhari:6922) and [Muslim 1676a](https://sunnah.com/muslim:1676a) with context. Research approved scholars’ definitions, evidentiary conditions, authority, repentance and distinctions between cases. Any claim that the rule concerns only political treason must be tested against the actual sources. Modern rights or state-law comparisons require current primary legal texts.

**Research difficulties:** Do not soften or hide the death-penalty material, or equate sincere questioning with a legal verdict. Separate what a source says, how it was interpreted and what a particular state enacted. No personal enforcement guidance or verdict about an individual belongs in the article. The moral and legal tension must be addressed directly.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

### 9. How should slavery in Islamic texts and history be understood?

**Reader concern:** Why do the texts recognise ownership of people, including sexual relations with enslaved women, and how does that relate to dignity and freedom?

**Overlap and distinct scope:** Article 12 distinguishes texts, law and historical conduct. Article 9’s spiritual standing does not answer ownership, consent or enslavement.

**Original research brief:** Map texts encouraging emancipation, including [90:11–16](https://quran.com/al-balad/11-16), alongside texts recognising ownership, including [23:5–6](https://quran.com/23/5-6). Research authenticated reports and approved explanations of acquisition, emancipation, treatment, sexual relations and relevant restrictions. Use primary documentary research for actual historical practices and official evidence for modern abolition and trafficking law if discussed.

**Research difficulties:** Do not equate enslavement with ordinary employment, suggest encouragement to free slaves was itself an immediate universal ban, or avoid the questions of coercion and consent. Historical improvements do not by themselves resolve the ethical objection. Define eras and legal categories; contemporary claims need current evidence. No practical enslavement guidance belongs here.

**Status:** Written in English, Arabic and German; editorial comparison complete; integrated locally as a draft. Independent acceptance remains open.

## Document checks and actual status

Checked on 19 October 2026: 21 articles in the agreed order; 63 language versions; 189 answered follow-ups (63 per language); 127 defined source references; matching reference-key sets across each article’s three versions; 171 valid internal/course links against actual course slugs; source URL syntax and reference resolution; all required article/review/brief sections; all nine briefs marked integrated locally; both inheritance examples’ arithmetic. Original SHA-256 hashes still match all 12 existing topic-answer JSON files. New articles do not require former website answers. Website and rendered interaction results are recorded separately in the local implementation record.

These checks establish document integrity, not religious accuracy, scientific completeness, historical certainty, native-language acceptance or publication approval. Source support and meaning were compared editorially while writing each article; external URLs were inspected during research rather than subjected to a separate exhaustive network-availability test. Little’s full thesis was inaccessible: only its repository abstract was assessed. Other specific research limits are retained per article. Local course URLs are preview links and need the website running on port 3000. They do not represent public deployment.

[Master manuscript](Bayan-Hard-Questions-master.md) · [Ordered review and expansion briefs](Bayan-Hard-Questions-review.md) · [Updated work plan](Bayan-work-plan.md)

## Release status

21 full drafts are available in three languages and integrated locally. Editorial comparison is complete with the specific gaps recorded above; this is not a claim that every philosophical, historical, scientific or legal issue has been settled. Independent scholarly review, native Arabic/German acceptance and reader feedback remain open. Production answer replacement, approval changes and public publication have not been performed. Historical and scientific sources here do not expand the live AI’s source library.

Quran wording in the articles is explanatory paraphrase, not a new translation. Quran.com provides the original Arabic and named translations shown on the source pages (English inspected with Dr Mustafa Khattab, The Clear Quran). Sunnah.com identifies its collection translations: Bukhari by Dr M. Muhsin Khan and Muslim by Abdul Hamid Siddiqui. Bayan’s Arabic and German prose are authored equivalents of the explanations, not unattributed translations of those published translations. Scholar views are paraphrased and attributed; no new view is selected by counting quotations.

## Subsequent local website implementation

All 21 authored articles (63 language versions) are integrated locally in the existing layout, with development-only draft access. Original 12 prepared answer files and all approvals remain unchanged. Each addition was completed in English and compared with Arabic/German before integration and moving to the next. Original 12 article bodies are retained except two stale expansion-brief pointers updated to existing articles. No database, live AI source, public deployment, commit or push changes.
